People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

It keeps coming back, and that scares me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I guess… about two months. Since we took on the mortgage, really. Before that I was kind of pushing hard with the business stuff, almost too much, and then it just… dropped into this low stretch.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange sensations, heard voices, or seen things that aren't there?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think so. I haven’t heard voices or seen anything like that. Just… my own thoughts, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past week where you felt somewhat better, or has it been consistently bad?\n\nPatient: It’s been pretty consistently bad. Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, flat feeling… and I’ll just sit at the kitchen table looking out at the yard, feeling like there isn’t really a good future ahead.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're moving or speaking more slowly than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. My husband says I’ve been moving around the house like I’m underwater. And sometimes when he asks me something simple, it takes me a bit to answer… like the words just come out really slow.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods in the last two months where your symptoms significantly worsened?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess around when we took on the mortgage, maybe two months ago. Before that I was really pushing hard, kind of overdoing the business stuff, and then it just… dropped into this low stretch.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: Not really restless, no. It’s more the opposite… my husband says I move around the house like I’m underwater. And when he asks me something simple, it takes me a while to answer, like the words come out slow.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you used to enjoy that you still find yourself looking forward to?\n\nPatient: Not really. I used to look forward to hiking, yoga… working in my garden, taking bird photos. Lately I just cancel, or I don’t even get the camera out of the closet. It all feels like too much effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fidgety or having trouble sitting still lately?\n\nPatient: No… not fidgety. If anything it’s slower. My husband says I move around like I’m underwater, and sometimes when he asks me something, it takes me a bit to get the words out.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving more slowly than usual most of the time over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. My husband’s noticed it more than I have. He says I move around the house like I’m underwater, and sometimes even answering a simple question takes me a while… like the words come out really slowly.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of sadness and emptiness are present most of the day, or are they more intermittent?\n\nPatient: Most of the day, I think. I usually wake up with this heavy, flat feeling… like it’s just sitting in my chest. Sometimes I’ll just sit at the kitchen table looking out at the yard and it feels like… there isn’t really a good version of what’s ahead.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past two months?\n\nPatient: Not that I’ve really noticed. I mean… my clothes don’t feel much different. I haven’t been tracking it closely.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I haven’t been weighing myself or anything… I haven’t noticed a clear change.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
